**PAPER PLAN:**

**§1. Preliminaries and notation.**
- $n \ge 2$; $\Bbbk = \mathbb{Q}(q)$; $q$ not a root of unity (transcendental). Actually $\Bbbk = k(q)$, $k$ any field of char 0, $q$ transcendental — or simply $\Bbbk = \mathbb{Q}(q)$.
- Weights: $\mu_i := e_i - \frac1n(e_1+\cdots+e_n) \in P$ (weight lattice of $\sl_n$); note $\sum_i \mu_i = 0$; simple roots $\alpha_i = \mu_i - \mu_{i+1}$.
- **The simply-connected quantum group** $U := U_q^{ext}(\sl_n)$ per [KS, Ch. 3/6?]: generators $\hat K_\mu$ ($\mu \in P$; multiplicative: $\hat K_\mu \hat K_\nu = \hat K_{\mu+\nu}$), $E_i, F_i$ ($1\le i\le n-1$), with relations: $\hat K_\mu E_j \hat K_\mu^{-1} = q^{(\mu,\alpha_j)}E_j$; $\hat K_\mu F_j \hat K_\mu^{-1} = q^{-(\mu,\alpha_j)}F_j$; $[E_i,F_j] = \delta_{ij}\frac{\hat K_{\alpha_i} - \hat K_{\alpha_i}^{-1}}{q-q^{-1}}$; quantum Serre. Write $\hat K_i := \hat K_{\mu_i}$; then $\hat K_1\cdots\hat K_n = 1$ and $\hat K_{\alpha_i} = \hat K_i\hat K_{i+1}^{-1}$.
- **Borel subalgebras**: $U_q(\fb_+) := \Bbbk\langle \hat K_\mu, E_i\rangle$, $U_q(\fb_-) := \Bbbk\langle\hat K_\mu, F_i\rangle$; these are Hopf subalgebras with the standard coproducts:
$\Delta(\hat K_\mu) = \hat K_\mu\otimes\hat K_\mu$, $\Delta(E_i) = E_i\otimes 1 + \hat K_{\alpha_i}\otimes E_i$, $\Delta(F_i) = F_i\otimes\hat K_{\alpha_i}^{-1} + 1\otimes F_i$. (Fix conventions!)

**§2. The Drinfeld–Tanisaki pairing.**
Define $\varphi: U_q(\fb_+)\times U_q(\fb_-) \to \Bbbk$ on generators: $\varphi(\hat K_i, \hat K_j) := q^{1/n - \delta_{ij}}$, $\varphi(E_i,F_j) = \delta_{ij}(q^{-1}-q)^{-1}$, $\varphi(\hat K_i, F_j) = 0 = \varphi(E_i, \hat K_j)$.
Note $\varphi(\hat K_i,\hat K_j) = q^{-(\mu_i,\mu_j)}$.

The presentation:

**Theorem (Presentation).** $D := U_q(\fb_-)\bowtie U_q(\fb_+)$ (as below) is generated by $\iota_-(\hat K'_\mu)$, $\iota_-(F_i)$, $\iota_+(\hat K_\mu)$, $\iota_+(E_i)$ with relations:
(D1) $\iota_\pm(\hat K_\mu)$'s all commute; $\iota_-(\hat K'_1\cdots\hat K'_n) = 1 = \iota_+(\hat K_1\cdots\hat H_n)$.
(D2) $\iota_+(\hat K_\mu)E_j\iota_+(\hat K_\mu)^{-1} = q^{(\mu,\alpha_j)}E_j$; $\iota_-(\hat K'_\mu)F_j\iota_-(\hat K'_\mu)^{-1} = q^{-(\mu,\alpha_j)}F_j$.

So: with $\fb_\pm^{pre}$ (smash-product Hopf algebras with built-in q-commutations), the pairing is well-defined by generator-values + axioms (consistency with built-in relations verified by induction as above), and the only remaining step for descending to $U_q(\fb_\pm)$ is Serre-annihilation. Then the pairing descends to $U_q(\fb_\pm)$: define $\varphi(\bar x, \bar y) := \tilde\varphi(x,y)$: well-defined since $\tilde\varphi$ annihilates the Serre ideals on either side ✓✓. $\mathscr{S}_n(\mathcal{B}^*)$ := the $\Bbbk$-algebra presented by:
- Generators: the diagonal arcs $b_{ii}^{\pm1}$, $c_{ii}^{\pm1}$ ($1\le i\le n$) and the simple arcs $b_{i+1,i}$, $c_{i,i+1}$ ($1\le i\le n-1$). [Diagrammatically: the bigon $\mathcal{B}$... the arcs directed rightward; I'll draw a TikZ picture.]

**Theorem 1 (= part (1))**: The assignment ... extends to an algebra isomorphism $\Theta:\mathscr{S}_n(\mathcal{B}^*)\to U_q(\fb_-)\bowtie U_q(\fb_+)$. Proof: immediate from the presentation theorem + relabeling (the relabeling $c_{ii}\mapsto\iota_+(\hat H_i)^{-1}$ is invertible ✓).

**Theorem 2 (= part (2))**: $\mathscr{S}_n(\mathcal{B}^*)/I\cong U_q(\sl_n) = U_q^{ext}(\sl_n)$, $I = \langle b_{ii}c_{ii}-1: 1\le i\le n\rangle$. Proof: via Theorem 1 + the quotient theorem ($D/J\cong U_q^{ext}(\sl_n)$), noting $\Theta(b_{ii}c_{ii}-1) = \iota_-(\hat K'_i)\iota_+(\hat H_i)^{-1}-1$ ✓.

**Relations of the double $D = U_q(\fb_-)\bowtie U_q(\fb_+)$** (with $\iota_+(E_i), \iota_+(\hat K_\mu)$ from $\fb_+$; $\iota_-(F_i), \iota_-(\hat K'_\mu)$ from $\fb_-$):

(D1) $\iota_+(\hat K_\mu)\iota_+(\hat K_\nu) = \iota_+(\hat K_{\mu+\nu})$ etc. (built into notation); $\iota_+(\hat K_1\cdots\hat K_n) = 1$, $\iota_-(\hat K'_1\cdots\hat K'_n) = 1$; $\iota_+(\hat K_\mu)\iota_-(\hat K'_\nu) = \iota_-(\hat K'_\nu)\iota_+(\hat K_\mu)$.

Abstract: state the two theorems.

§1 Introduction: context; the two results; relation to literature ([Z] for $\sl_2$ double-of-Borel and quotient; [UvA] for the double-as-braided-Hopf-algebra and the $sl_2$ presentation; [Cam] for presentations by Borels; [hal] for the algebraic Drinfeld double & YBE; [KS97] for $U_q^{ext}$ and the pairing; [Dr87] original). Outline of proof.

§2 Conventions: $\Bbbk = \mathbb{Q}(q)$; $q$ indeterminate. Lattice data. Hopf pairing definition. Definition of generalized quantum double $D_\varphi(A,B)$ (first factor $A$, second $B$, pairing $\varphi: A\times B$, straightening rule) — cite [UvA, §1], [hal, §1.4], [Majid], [Kassel], [Dr87], [Tak?]. Statement: $A$, $B$ embed, basis $A\otimes B$ (cite/obvious by construction). Remark: braided with R-matrix [UvA Thm 1.7].

§3 The simply-connected quantum group and its Borels: Definition $U_q(\sl(n))$ (U1)-(U4); remark on KS's $U_q^{ext}$ and simply-connectedness; Hopf structure with coproducts; Borels $U_q(\fb_\pm)$ with Hopf structures; PBW proposition (cite).

§4 The Drinfeld–Tanisaki skew pairing: Theorem 4.1: existence/uniqueness. Proof with Lemmas 4.2 (pre-algebras & extension), 4.3 (monomial pairings: length-1,2,3 formulas with proofs), 4.4 (Serre annihilation: the three sums), 4.5 (descent). Also Corollary: the values match the question's.

§5 The double of the Borels: Definition 5.1: $D := D_\varphi(U_q(\fb_+), U_q(\fb_-))$, denoted $U_q(\fb_-)\bowtie U_q(\fb_+)$ (remark on notation). Lemma 5.2: cross-relations (computations (i) torus-torus, (ii) torus-E, (iii) torus-F, (iv) E-F) — each a short computation mirroring [UvA]. Theorem 5.4: presentation (D1)-(D5); proof: spanning induction + basis comparison. Corollary 5.5: $D$ has PBW basis.

§6 The arc algebra and the isomorphism (Theorem 1 of the question): Figure; Definition 6.1 ($\mathscr{S}_n(\mathcal{B}^*)$ with (S1)-(S4)); composite arcs Remark 6.2 (LS); Theorem 6.3: $\Theta$ iso; proof.

§7 The quotient (Theorem 2 of the question): Theorem 7.1: $D/\langle\iota_-(\hat K'_i)\iota_+(\hat K_i)^{-1}-1\rangle\cong U_q(\sl(n))$; proof: two maps. Theorem 7.2: $\mathscr{S}_n(\mathcal{B}^*)/I\cong U_q(\sl(n))$, $I = \langle b_{ii}c_{ii}-1\rangle$; proof via §6.

§8 Concluding remarks: R-matrix/braided [UvA Thm 1.7; hal]; generalization outlook to arbitrary Cartan data [cf. Z]; modules [cf. Z].
